//! Owner-mediated release publication in the running HTTP process.
use super::*;
use crate::release_control::Command;
#[cfg(unix)]
use crate::release_control::Listener;
use snapback4_core::engine::release::ReleaseActivation;

#[cfg(unix)]
pub(super) fn start(
    shared: Arc<Shared>,
    dir: std::path::PathBuf,
) -> Result<std::thread::JoinHandle<()>, String> {
    let store = shared
        .owner
        .as_ref()
        .ok_or("release control requires a persisted owner")?
        .path()
        .map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
    let listener = Listener::bind(&dir, store)?;
    crate::lifecycle::managed_cleanup();
    crate::lifecycle::background("release-control", move || {
        listener.run(|command| apply(&shared, &dir, command))
    })
}

// @ref LLP 3000.000.000#5-hot-activation-and-ownership — only assignments and committed rebind follow the database commit.
pub(super) fn apply(
    shared: &Shared,
    dir: &std::path::Path,
    command: Command,
) -> Result<Json, String> {
    match command {
        Command::Status {
            after,
            items_after,
            event,
        } => {
            let mut engine=lock(&shared.engine);
            let mut status=engine.store_mut().release_status(after,items_after.as_deref(),event).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?;
            status["clock"]=json!({"now":shared.now(),"offsetMs":lock(&shared.clock).offset()});
            Ok(status)
        },
        Command::Prune {
            confirm,
            expected_revision,
        } => {
            let mut result = lock(&shared.engine)
                .store_mut()
                .prune_dormant(confirm, Some(expected_revision))
                .map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
            if let Some(items) = result["items"].as_array_mut() {
                items.truncate(16);
            }
            result["items_after"] = result["items"]
                .as_array()
                .and_then(|items| items.last())
                .map(|i| i["key"].clone())
                .unwrap_or(Json::Null);
            Ok(result)
        }
        Command::Activate {
            release,
            expected_release,
            expected_revision,
            digest,
            rollback,
            restore_dormant,
            allow_feed_adoption,
        } => {
            if release == 0 {
                return Err("release id must be positive".into());
            }
            let project = Project {
                root: dir.join("releases").join(release.to_string()),
            };
            if !std::fs::symlink_metadata(&project.root)
                .is_ok_and(|m| m.is_dir() && !m.file_type().is_symlink())
            {
                return Err(format!(
                    "release {release} is not an immutable release directory"
                ));
            }
            if crate::release_files::digest(&project.root)? != digest {
                return Err("staged release content changed; redeploy its exact source".into());
            }
            if project.sources().map_err(|e| e.to_string())?.is_empty() {
                return Err(format!(
                    "release {release} has no Quarry source; select a complete immutable release"
                ));
            }
            let bound = project.compile().map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
            if !bound.diagnostics.is_empty() {
                return Err(bound
                    .diagnostics
                    .iter()
                    .map(ToString::to_string)
                    .collect::<Vec<_>>()
                    .join("\n"));
            }
            let prepared = crate::native::prepare(&project, &bound).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
            crate::dev_worker::prepare_release(&project, &bound.schema)?;
            crate::release_files::link_store(&project.root)?;
            if crate::release_files::digest(&project.root)? != digest {
                return Err("release artifacts changed during preparation; retry after inspecting immutable source".into());
            }
            crate::release_files::durable(&project.root)?;
            let mut pointer =
                crate::release_files::Pointer::prepare(dir, release, expected_release, rollback)?;
            let options = ReleaseActivation {
                release,
                expected_previous: expected_release,
                expected_revision: Some(expected_revision),
                restore_dormant,
                native_digest: prepared.installed.as_ref().map(|m| m.digest.clone()),
                operation: if rollback { "rollback" } else { "deploy" }.into(),
            };
            let _requests=shared.release_requests.write();
            let mut engine = lock(&shared.engine);
            lock(&shared.readers).begin_adoption(&bound);
            let generation = match engine.adopt_release_at(
                bound.schema.clone(),
                bound.programs.iter().map(|p| p.program.clone()),
                &options,
                allow_feed_adoption,
                shared.now(),
            ) {
                Ok(generation) => generation,
                Err(error) => {
                    lock(&shared.readers).finish_adoption();
                    return Err(error.to_string());
                }
            };
            #[cfg(test)]
            test_work(&shared.release_committed);
            engine.set_prepared_native(prepared.module);
            engine.set_presence(Some(shared.presence.clone()));
            shared.refresh_time_audience_clock(&engine, shared.now());
            lock(&shared.readers).replace(&engine, &bound.refusal_sites);
            {
                let mut active = lock(&shared.bound);
                let mut advertised = lock(&shared.generation);
                let mut native = lock(&shared.native);
                shared.latency.adopt(&bound);
                *active = bound;
                *advertised = generation;
                *native = prepared.installed;
                let mut root = lock(&shared.release_root);
                *root = Some(project.root);
                shared.worker_revision.fetch_add(1, Ordering::Release);
            }
            crate::lifecycle::generation(generation);
            shared.publish_engine(&engine);
            // Completion I/O can fail, but never abandons the new runtime.
            pointer.finish(generation)?;
            snapback4_core::diagnostic!(
                "snapback4 serve: release {release} activated without restart as generation {generation}"
            );
            Ok(
                json!({"release":release,"generation":generation,"revision":expected_revision+1,"hot":true,"adoption":engine.store().adoption_report.compact(),"details":engine.store().adoption_report.summary(),"backfills":engine.store().adoption_report.backfills}),
            )
        }
    }
}
//! Server activation builder; all fallible work precedes commit.
use super::*;
impl Engine {
    pub(super) fn activate(
        store: &mut Store,
        schema: &Schema,
        programs: &BTreeMap<String, Program>,
        release: Option<&release::ReleaseActivation>,
        now: i64,
        allow_feed_adoption: bool,
        expected_release: Option<u64>,
        expected_backend: Option<&crate::store::opening::Backend>,
    ) -> Result<(u64, crate::store::opening::Metadata), RuntimeError> {
        schema.validate_column_owners()?;
        for program in programs.values() {
            crate::schema::ephemeral::admit_program(schema, program)?;
            admission::check_state_predicates(schema, program)?;
        }
        for program in programs.values() {
            for table in crate::ir::read_sites(program)
                .into_iter()
                .map(|s| s.table)
                .chain(crate::ir::write_tables(program).into_iter().map(|(t, _)| t))
            {
                schema.refuse_private(&table)?;
            }
        }
        for schedule in &schema.schedules {
            if !schedule.valid()
            {
                return Err(RuntimeError::Schema(format!(
                    "schedule {} has an invalid period or offset",
                    schedule.name
                )));
            }
            let Some(program) = programs
                .get(&schedule.target)
                .filter(|p| matches!(p.kind, ProgramKind::Job | ProgramKind::Action))
            else {
                return Err(RuntimeError::Schema(format!(
                    "schedule {} names missing job or action {}; restore or remove the schedule",
                    schedule.name, schedule.target
                )));
            };
            if program.kind == ProgramKind::Action {
                let seat = schedule
                    .service
                    .as_deref()
                    .filter(|seat| crate::identity::is_service(seat))
                    .ok_or_else(|| {
                        RuntimeError::Schema(
                            "a scheduled action requires its declared service seat".into(),
                        )
                    })?;
                actions::authorize(program, seat)?;
            } else if schedule.service.is_some() {
                return Err(RuntimeError::Schema(
                    "a scheduled job cannot name a service seat".into(),
                ));
            }
            if program.args.iter().any(|(name, kind)| {
                !matches!(name.as_str(), "periodStart" | "periodEnd")
                    || *kind != crate::ir::ArgType::Time
            }) {
                return Err(RuntimeError::Schema(
                    "scheduled work takes periodStart: time and periodEnd: time, or nothing".into(),
                ));
            }
        }
        let prior_items = std::cell::RefCell::new(Vec::new());
        let population=std::cell::Cell::new(None);
        let outcome=store.activate_at_with_launch(schema, Some(programs), false, now, allow_feed_adoption, |conn| {
            Store::check_selected_release(conn, expected_release)?;
            if let Some(backend) = expected_backend { backend.check(conn)?; }
            if let Some(options)=release {
                options.check(conn)?;
                *prior_items.borrow_mut()=crate::store::release_history::inventory(conn,None,usize::MAX)?;
                crate::store::dormant::enable(conn)?;
                conn.execute("INSERT INTO _meta(key,value) VALUES('release_native_digest',?1) ON CONFLICT(key) DO UPDATE SET value=excluded.value",[options.native_digest.as_deref().unwrap_or("")])?;
                conn.execute("INSERT INTO _meta(key,value) VALUES('activation_restore',?1) ON CONFLICT(key) DO UPDATE SET value=excluded.value",[if options.restore_dormant {"true"}else{"false"}])?;
            }
            Ok(())
        }, |tx, changed, report| {
            let now=now.max(tx.time_audience_clock()?);
            if let Some(options)=release {tx.set_meta("release",&options.release.to_string())?;}
            use rusqlite::OptionalExtension;
            let repair_live_sums = tx.conn().query_row("SELECT value FROM _meta WHERE key = 'live_sum_candidates'", [], |row| row.get::<_, String>(0)).optional()?.as_deref() != Some("1");
            let previous: Option<String> = tx.conn().query_row("SELECT schema FROM _generations WHERE generation = (SELECT CAST(value AS INTEGER) FROM _meta WHERE key = 'generation')", [], |row| row.get(0)).optional()?;
            let previous: Option<Schema> = previous.map(|text| serde_json::from_str(&text).map_err(|e| RuntimeError::decode(e, RuntimeError::Store))).transpose()?;
            let needs_maintained_id_marker = (schema.maintains.iter().any(|maintain| maintain.row_key.is_none())
                || previous.as_ref().is_some_and(|old| old.maintains.iter().any(|maintain| maintain.row_key.is_none())))
                && tx.meta(crate::schema::MAINTAINED_ID_META)?.as_deref() != Some(crate::schema::MAINTAINED_ID_VERSION);
            if !changed && !repair_live_sums && !needs_maintained_id_marker { return Ok(()); }
            let repair_maintained_ids = needs_maintained_id_marker
                && previous.as_ref().is_some_and(|old| old.maintains.iter().any(|maintain| maintain.row_key.is_none()));
            if changed {
                let epoch = tx.next_seq()?;
                for table in partition::changed_group_audiences(previous.as_ref(), schema) {
                    tx.set_meta(&partition::audience_epoch_key(&table), &epoch.to_string())?;
                    tx.set_meta(&partition::audience_epoch_time_key(&table), &now.to_string())?;
                }
            }
            let population_changed=tx.meta("activation_population")?.as_deref()==Some("true");
            let rebuild: Vec<_> = schema.maintains.iter().filter(|m| {
                (changed && (population_changed || previous.as_ref().is_none_or(|old| crate::successor::maintain_rebuild_needed(old, schema, m))))
                    || (repair_maintained_ids && m.row_key.is_none())
                    || (repair_live_sums && matches!(m.kind, crate::schema::MaintainKind::Sum { .. }) && schema.table(&m.source).is_some_and(|source| source.is_expiring()))
            }).collect();
            tx.set_meta("live_sum_candidates", "1")?;
            if needs_maintained_id_marker {
                // This write is inside the activation transaction: a failed
                // rebuild leaves both old rows and the old/missing marker. A
                // first aggregate has no legacy target identity to rewrite,
                // but records the current address function for later opens.
                tx.set_meta(crate::schema::MAINTAINED_ID_META, crate::schema::MAINTAINED_ID_VERSION)?;
            }
            let attribution = recorded::paths_changed(schema, previous.as_ref());
            let columns = column_activation::changed_parents(schema, if population_changed {None} else {previous.as_ref()});
            if rebuild.is_empty() && !attribution && columns.is_empty() && !repair_maintained_ids { return Ok(()); }
            let seq = tx.next_seq()?;
            let phase = std::time::Instant::now();
            if attribution {
                // This runner owns only activation rewrites; ordinary rebuilds
                // below retain their commit and partition accounting.
                let mut capture = Runner::new(tx, schema, Context::system().at(now), Meter::new(Ceilings::unbounded(), Cut::Abort), seq);
                capture.capture_activation(previous.as_ref(), Some(report))?;
                report.detail("capture recorded paths", phase);
                // A newly introduced aggregate has no legacy identity map to
                // preserve. Keep its event-free recorded-path rebuild out of
                // commit accounting, as activation did before the address
                // migration existed.
                if !repair_maintained_ids {
                    capture.rebuild_recorded_activation(previous.as_ref(), &rebuild.iter().copied().filter(|m| m.private.is_none()).collect::<Vec<_>>(), report)?;
                }
            }
            let mut runner = Runner::new(tx, schema, Context::system().at(now), Meter::new(Ceilings::unbounded(), Cut::Abort), seq);
            if repair_maintained_ids {
                let started = std::time::Instant::now();
                let rewritten = runner.rewrite_legacy_maintained_references(&rebuild.iter().copied().cloned().collect::<Vec<_>>(), previous.as_ref(), Some(tx))?;
                if rewritten != 0 { report.changes.push(format!("rewrote {rewritten} legacy aggregate references")); }
                report.detail("rewrite legacy aggregate references", started);
            }
            // Legacy maps must be captured before any activation rebuild can
            // remove their target rows. Recorded values themselves are
            // stamped first so their historical paths may still traverse the
            // predecessor addresses.
            if attribution && repair_maintained_ids {
                runner.rebuild_recorded_activation(previous.as_ref(), &rebuild.iter().copied().filter(|m| m.private.is_none()).collect::<Vec<_>>(), report)?;
            }
            runner.activate_columns(previous.as_ref(), &rebuild, &columns, Some(report))?;
            for maintain in rebuild.into_iter().filter(|m| m.private.is_none()) {
                if !recorded::table_paths_changed(schema.table(&maintain.source), previous.as_ref().and_then(|s| s.table(&maintain.source))) {
                    runner.rebuild_maintain_reported(maintain, report)?;
                }
            }
            let events = runner.events.take();
            let tables: Vec<String> = runner.written_tables.iter().cloned().collect();
            let written = runner.rows_written;
            drop(runner);
            if written > 0 || !columns.is_empty() {
                tx.record_commit(seq, now, "rebuild", crate::SYSTEM_PRINCIPAL, "rebuild", &tables, written)?;
                partition::append(tx, seq, &events)?;
            }
            Ok(())
        }, |tx| {
            if let Some(options)=release {
                crate::store::release_history::finalize_restorations(tx)?;
                let mut items=crate::store::release_history::inventory(tx.conn(),None,usize::MAX)?;
                let dormant_items=items.len();
                let mut restored_items=0usize;
                for item in &mut items {
                    let before=prior_items.borrow().iter().find(|i|i.key==item.key).cloned();
                    let count=before.as_ref().map_or(0,|i|i.rows);
                    item.before_rows=Some(count);item.after_rows=Some(item.rows);
                    item.before_sample_ids=Some(before.map_or_else(Vec::new,|i|i.sample_ids));
                    let restored=if item.kind=="rows" {
                        tx.meta(&format!("activation_narrowed_restored:{}",item.path))?.and_then(|s|s.parse::<u64>().ok()).unwrap_or(0)
                    } else if item.kind=="column" {tx.meta(&format!("activation_column_restored:{}",item.path))?.and_then(|s|s.parse::<u64>().ok()).unwrap_or(0)} else {0};
                    item.restored_rows=Some(restored);
                    if restored>0 {item.action="partially restored".into();restored_items+=1;}
                }
                let remaining:std::collections::BTreeSet<_>=items.iter().map(|i|i.key.clone()).collect();
                for mut item in prior_items.borrow().iter().filter(|i|!remaining.contains(&i.key)).cloned() {
                    let restored=if matches!(item.kind.as_str(),"rows"|"column") {
                        let prefix=if item.kind=="rows" {"activation_narrowed_restored"} else {"activation_column_restored"};
                        tx.meta(&format!("{prefix}:{}",item.path))?.and_then(|s|s.parse::<u64>().ok()).unwrap_or(0)
                    } else if item.kind=="table" {
                        tx.meta(&format!("activation_table_restored:{}",item.path))?.and_then(|s|s.parse::<u64>().ok()).unwrap_or(0)
                    } else {item.rows};
                    item.action=if restored>0 || item.definition_only {"restored"} else {"removed without restoration"}.into();
                    item.before_rows=Some(item.rows);item.after_rows=Some(0);item.restored_rows=Some(restored);item.before_sample_ids=Some(item.sample_ids.clone());
                    if restored>0 || item.definition_only {restored_items+=1;}
                    items.push(item);
                }
                population.set(Some((tx.meta("activation_narrowed_retained")?.and_then(|s|s.parse::<u64>().ok()).unwrap_or(0),tx.meta("activation_narrowed_restored")?.and_then(|s|s.parse::<u64>().ok()).unwrap_or(0))));
                crate::store::release_history::event(tx,&options.operation,Some(options.release),options.expected_previous,serde_json::json!({"dormant_items":dormant_items,"restored_items":restored_items,"native_digest":options.native_digest,"narrowed_retained":tx.meta("activation_narrowed_retained")?.and_then(|s|s.parse::<u64>().ok()).unwrap_or(0),"narrowed_restored":tx.meta("activation_narrowed_restored")?.and_then(|s|s.parse::<u64>().ok()).unwrap_or(0)}),&items)?;
                tx.conn().execute("DELETE FROM _meta WHERE key IN ('activation_restore','activation_population','activation_narrowed_retained','activation_narrowed_restored') OR key LIKE 'activation_narrowed_restored:%' OR key LIKE 'activation_column_restored:%' OR key LIKE 'activation_table_restored:%' OR key LIKE 'activation_table_restored_ids:%' OR key LIKE 'activation_narrowed_restored_ids:%' OR key LIKE 'activation_column_restored_ids:%'",[])?;
            }
            crate::store::opening::Metadata::read(tx)
        });
        if outcome.is_ok() {
            if let Some((retained,restored))=population.get().filter(|(a,b)|*a>0 || *b>0) {
                store.adoption_report.changes.insert(0,format!("{retained} newly dormant {}; {restored} narrowed {} restored", if retained == 1 { "row" } else { "rows" }, if restored == 1 { "row" } else { "rows" }));
            }
        }
        if outcome.is_ok() {
            if let Ok(items)=store.dormant_inventory(None,usize::MAX) {
                if release.is_some() { store.adoption_report.changes.insert(0, crate::store::adoption_report::AdoptionReport::dormant(&items)); }
            }
        }
        outcome
    }
}

# LLP 3000.000.000: Retained storage and hot release activation

**Type:** RFC
**Status:** Implemented
**Systems:** Schema evolution, Storage, CLI, Deployment, Replica generations
**Author:** Codex for Charlie Cheever (Workstream ROLL)
**Date:** 2026-10-12
**Revised:** 2026-10-18 (DAY5 wave-15 amendments); 2026-10-16 (PREV wave-13 preview decisions §13); 2026-10-15 (SURF readiness/closeout; aggregate identity migration covers retained references; PUBFIX28: consistent disjoint preview prose and blocking accepted control streams); 2026-10-14 (NARR wave-11 amendment); 2026-10-13 (§10: REL2 wave-10 release lifecycle); 2026-10-13 (MACFIX: explicit pre-retention compatibility boundary and canonical deployment/worker paths); 2026-10-12 (§5.1: path-independent control socket; implemented; dual-family confirmation, captured lifetimes, native generations, hot activation and complete qualification)
**Related:** [LLP 3000](./3000-snapback4.explainer.md), [LLP 3000.000](./3000.000-atomic-schema-and-release-activation.research.md), [LLP 3000.007](./3000.007-relaxed-quarry-and-explicit-residency.rfc.md), [LLP 3000.019](./3000.019-what-the-builder-still-wrote.rfc.md)

Staging store classification and real-user provisioning: [LLP 3000.000.000.001](./3000.000.000.001-staging-with-real-users.rfc.md) (STAGE).

## 1. Commission and scope

Charlie approved the ideal end-game release lifecycle and commissioned implementation on 2026-10-12. Implementer: this ROLL session, beginning that date. Wave 8 DT1 found additive rollback deletes new storage in all 50 day-two lanes; DT1a found unnamed enum conflicts; DT10 found deployment requires stopping serve. This supersedes the release-only removal semantics in LLP 3000.000's implementation follow-up. Development and device replacement keep their existing projection/reset semantics. No new publication authority or gate is introduced.

## 2. Retain by default

Every release activation (deploy and rollback) retains removed durable application tables, columns and index definitions in SQLite-owned dormant storage within the same activation transaction. The active r/i image remains an exact projection of the active schema. Dormant data is outside that namespace and unavailable to programs, rules, references, queries, sync, SQL application projections and clients. The Quarry/IR binder admits only declared application table names; it has no general application SQL evaluator. Dormant internal names are not added to Schema, and schema validation forbids declarations in the reserved `_dormant_` and `_release_` namespaces. Inspection `snapback4 sql` is owner-only and may enumerate it. A future application SQL adapter must keep its declared authorized input allow-list. Asset reads must resolve the home table, column and row in the active schema/r image and check the current read rule; a dormant home refuses E_ASSET_UNKNOWN even when a caller knows its id. Test the reserved-name/program boundary and direct fetch by id. Reintroducing a dormant name deliberately reactivates its data, after full validation under the candidate schema. A deployment that reuses any dormant name refuses unless --restore-dormant explicitly selects restoration; its refusal lists retained items/counts/samples and the alternatives `deploy <dir> --restore-dormant` or `prune <dir> --allow-destructive` before fresh creation. Rollback's selection of an immutable older release explicitly selects restoration; `rollback <dir> --to N` also selects an immutable release for deliberate roll forward. To convert durable to ephemeral, deploy a release without that table, prune it explicitly, then deploy the ephemeral definition. This applies to deployments as well as rollback; a rename remains two names, without an inferred data move.

Inventory and audit entries carry a stable typed key (`table:name`, `column:table.name`, or `index:table.name`); pagination uses the returned key, not a bare path that could collide between columns and indexes. The SQLite format is `_dormant_tables(table_name, definition)` for whole-table Table definitions, `_dormant_rows(t,id,data)` for their stored images, `_dormant_columns(table_name,column_name,definition)` for retired column type/default/ownership, `_dormant_values(t,id,column_name,data)` for their cells, `_dormant_indexes(table_name,index_name,definition)` for index definitions and `_dormant_refs(t,id,column_name,parent_t,parent_id,invalid)` for deletion evidence with a parent lookup index. Catalog numbers are reserved monotonically and reused for the same physical name, but dormant rows are never kept in r under a hidden catalog number. These tables are created only during successful release activation; legacy deployments start empty and cannot recover data previously deleted by older binaries. A successful release activation writes the release-only format marker `version=snapback4-release-store-3`. New Store open/version checks accept this, the prior snapback4-release-store-2 and the legacy snapback4-store-1; old binaries reject it with E_STORE rather than opening it. Development and device stores retain snapback4-store-1 and their formats are unchanged. The format marker rolls back with failed activation. On restore, omitted cells from whole-table images move to dormant column cells before projection, merging previous retired cells; full-table entries are consumed only on commit.

The compatibility boundary is the store format, independently of schema
capabilities. Published 0.2.33 refuses retained release-store versions 2 and 3
with `E_STORE: store version <version> is not snapback4-store-1; this data
directory belongs to another Snapback` (the read-only doctor form omits
the final data-directory explanation), including capability-free releases.
That refusal must preserve committed state and never interpret dormant data
under the old layout. Capability-free backend/schema bytes and unchanged
store-1 development/device fixtures retain their separate compatibility
controls (LLP 3000.022 §14.3); they do not establish old-server readability of
new release stores. The Darwin publication fence checks both boundaries.
The MACFIX compatibility/path amendment passed independent final
[Muse](./reviews/3000.000.000-macfix-r3.muse.md) and
[Codex](./reviews/3000.000.000-macfix-r3.codex.md) reviews at e9150bd171.
The required Darwin schema and worker files passed alone with default TMPDIR
(10 and 82 tests); whole-publication qualification is a separate claim.

Dormant whole tables store their row images; dormant columns store values keyed by table/column/row id and their definitions. Capture interpreted literal defaults and optional nulls when a column becomes dormant, preserving the value existing rows actually saw. Keep index definitions as history; preserve their data logically and rebuild physical keys on reactivation from current rows. Dormant indexes are never consulted for active writes, uniqueness or caps. Thus a new duplicate under the older release can precisely refuse forward activation. Derived rows are caches and are rebuilt, not authoritative history. Retention must precede existing derived-state retirement. An unsupported durable-to-ephemeral conversion refuses rather than silently discarding stored data.

Partial restoration consumes only candidate-visible cells: B's T(a,b) → absent T → C's T(a) restores a and moves b to dormant cells, with b's definition, then B restores b. For an identical (t,id,column) cell key, the newly retired active value wins: it is the most recent authoritative value. Preserve other dormant keys. Use transactional upsert and test retire → restore → update → retire → restore. Keep zero-row table/column/index definitions too. Whole-table restoration first restores its stored image, splits omitted cells into dormancy, then projects the active image. Consumption shares commit with validation. Refuse application-table to derived-table ownership changes, or authoritative-column to maintained/captured ownership changes, including when the application table/column was dormant. Derived caches never overwrite retained authoritative data. The refusal names the incompatible owner and directs a deliberate rename or prune while dormant.

At retirement record reference edges for dormant rows/cells (including optional/list references), bound to their current parent lifetime. An active delete transaction marks matching dormant edges invalid before the id can be reused, even for identical replacement values. Only a move into dormant storage or replacement of a derived cache is exempt from deletion invalidation. Actual authoritative-row deletion always invalidates matching dormant edges, including expiry during activation; failed activation rolls the invalidation back. A fixed-size identity migration rewrites dormant whole rows, cells, narrowed rows and their edge ledger when the referenced derived target lifetime survives migration; removing that target retires its lifetime, so its dormant edges remain bound to the retired address and restoration refuses rather than attaching them to a later reintroduction. Restoration refuses invalidated edges or missing parents with table/column/parent ids, counts and samples; it cannot silently recapture another parent's audience. For read-through rows, compare preserved captured audience and identity fields to the candidate parent before existing recapture work. A changed audience refuses restoration; inherited deadlines keep their original capture. Newly expired rows remain logically invisible and may be swept only under the reactivated schema. Test deletion/reuse, identical replacement, changed audience, nested references and expiry. This adds no device incarnation format.

Restoration, candidate validation, derived rebuild, catalog/generation/programs and current release commit together. Any error rolls all of them back. Retained values overwrite no explicit candidate writes: restoration precedes activation builders. Incompatible name reuse must pass candidate types and constraints; no flag bypasses validation.

## 3. Older writes and incompatible values

Updating an active row while its newer columns are dormant preserves those dormant values. Deleting that row deletes its dormant column values in the same transaction: forward never resurrects a deleted row, including when its id is reused. New rows written under the older schema have no dormant value. On forward they receive the candidate literal default or optional null; required columns without such a default refuse with exact missing-row counts and up to five ids. Repair by deploying a bridge schema with that column optional or a literal default using --restore-dormant, fill it through a bridge mutation, then deploy the desired required schema. Alternatively explicitly delete the named rows through the current release and retry. Never tell the older schema to write a field it lacks. Contextual now/viewer defaults do not invent historical values. Existing required-column backfill rules still apply.

Release activations retain incompatible existing values per row rather than refusing a type narrowing. Section 9 specifies the visibility, reference and restoration rules. Development adoption keeps its checked narrowing behavior. Missing newly required fields still need the bridge described above; retention applies to present historical values, not absent backfill. No implicit coercion or fallback is introduced.

For every data-dependent refusal, aggregate count and bounded samples (five distinct values/keys, 256 UTF-8 bytes each, plus truncation markers) at the failed column/index/constraint, without unbounded row retention. Include actual conflicting values for column failures, index name and conflicting keys for unique failures, and actionable command or schema repair. Detailed values are privileged operator diagnostics only, never ordinary application refusal payloads, query meter diagnostics or sync refusals. No claim that a green read-only preview freezes later writes: adoption rechecks under its transaction.

## 4. Explicit pruning and audit

snapback4 prune <dir> runs through the serving owner when live, otherwise the offline owner. It lists dormant tables, columns and indexes with row counts, logical serialized dormant bytes (including retained asset bytes separately) and at most five ids; it changes nothing without --allow-destructive. With that flag it permanently deletes exactly the currently dormant storage in one writer transaction, records a durable prune event, and leaves active rows/programs untouched. It releases dormant asset homes for the ordinary collector. Deploy/rollback --allow-destructive do not prune; direct users to the separate command. Prune intentionally deletes all dormant items as one reviewed storage boundary; per-item dependency-aware pruning is deferred because partial pruning can strand dormant references and asset homes. There is no implicit size or age collector: previews make storage growth visible without inventing a data-loss policy. After prune, reintroducing the schema gets empty tables and normal required-column/default behavior. Pruning is an irreversible operator action; the command output names every item dropped.

Release activation events live in the database transaction with activation revision, operation, release/previous/generation, timestamp and retained/restored summary. Prune records its item summary in that transaction. Existing releases.jsonl and current remain checked convenience copies; post-commit filesystem errors explicitly say activation succeeded. History is never edited or rewound. Rollback picks an earlier immutable source, not a historical data snapshot.

Dormant bytes have no automatic age-based deletion: retention lasts until explicit prune, and prune preview exposes populations so operators control disk use. Dormant asset homes remain live in the asset ledger but are unreadable because the active schema has no home. Active-row deletion also retires any associated dormant asset home. Dormant rows do not run old expiry or jobs while dormant; candidate liveness applies again upon reactivation. Explicit application deletion and ordinary active expiry still mean deletion, not an undo archive.

## 5. Hot activation and ownership

serve owns the database sidecar for its lifetime and publishes a Unix-domain release-control socket in the private per-user runtime directory (§5.1; directory 0700, socket 0600). Check peer credentials on both ends (Linux SO_PEERCRED, Darwin getpeereid), requiring the effective UID. Only the host filesystem owner has this capability; it is not an HTTP door, browser origin, bearer service seat or remote endpoint. Check filesystem objects before replacing a stale socket; never follow symlinks or replace a regular file. Use `.release-command` (a hardened private regular file, no-follow, single link) for the command flock and the deterministic runtime address (§5.1) for the control socket. lstat socket entries; only a same-owner socket may be removed while holding the exclusive store sidecar. Bound command JSON to 4096 bytes and response JSON to 64 KiB, with a 5-second input timeout and 120-second command response timeout. The trusted same-UID operator can directly edit source/database; malicious same-UID edits are outside the threat model, but ordinary source races are caught by a candidate content digest over source/native/worker files in the request and verified by the server. Numeric release id is the only source selector; never a caller path. Commands hold a separate private command flock while allocating/staging immutable release source. They acquire the store owner when stopped; E_STORE_BUSY with a running serve uses the local socket instead of opening another writer. Dev and an older/unreachable server continue to refuse with an exact stop/retry command.

snapback4 releases <dir> reads authoritative current release, activation revision, generation and durable events without adopting source, including while serve is live. This is the recovery command for an ambiguous result. A bounded command message identifies the owned database inode/canonical identity, candidate release id, expected current release and monotone activation revision, operation and explicit flags. The server compiles only its own immutable release directory, prepares native module/worker prerequisites before commit, then holds its existing engine writer guard for activation and in-memory publication. The server verifies both expected current release and activation revision inside the activation transaction. Increment activation revision on every successful release activation, including an equal-hash source change; A→B→A cannot satisfy an old command. Concurrent commands serialize on the command flock; out-of-band stale commands fail compare-and-swap. No request may choose arbitrary filesystem source. Compilation/validation failure leaves the previous engine, bound schema, native module, worker revision and reader factory unchanged.

A query already running in a pinned SQLite read transaction finishes on its old snapshot and reports that generation. A writer already executing finishes before activation obtains its engine guard. Queued HTTP operations are admitted under one release request guard covering signature lookup, argument decoding and execution. Activation acquires its exclusive side before taking the engine guard; admitted operations finish before activation, and later operations dispatch under the new generation. Internal detached readers still fence obsolete identities with E_GENERATION. Fresh requests after publication use the new engine; a stale pooled reader not yet pinned retries through the replacement factory. Prepare a runtime bundle containing bound programs, precompiled native module/metadata, worker root and validated named declarations; prepare the reader factory from the post-activation engine while its writer guard blocks new writers (reader opening failure after commit clears the old factory and uses existing lazy retry, never aborts a committed activation or advertises the old backend). Swap bound/generation/native under the common bound guard in the existing /schema guard order, retire pooled readers, rebind ephemeral state, invalidate change answers, wake sync/polls and update worker revision while holding the writer guard. The supervisor explicitly switches its immutable release project root and named declarations on revision, including addition/removal/replacement; externally supplied --worker stays an operator-owned entry. Worker preparation captures an immutable (root, entry, declarations/environment, supervisor revision) tuple. Under the engine guard it compares that captured revision to the current worker revision before minting credentials; stale preparation is discarded and rediscovered. Never relabel an old prepared source with the latest revision. Credentials bind that checked revision so later activation fences them even when spawn finishes later. Workers restart using the candidate packaged source and native continuation hashes keep the existing conservative parking policy. Long validation can pause writes; hot means no process restart, not an unbounded zero-pause latency promise.

The server continues an admitted activation even if the client hits its 120-second response timeout or closes the socket; failure to write the reply is not permission to roll back or exit the server. The database is authoritative at crash or lost socket reply. Timeout/EOF after sending a command reports an ambiguous result and instructs inspection of current and durable history; never silently resend as a new release or claim failure left the old release active. Before activation fsync required immutable source/native/worker files and their directories, then the published releases directory entry. The supported guarantee covers process crashes and filesystem/SQLite durability on hosts honoring fsync; no guarantee covers failing storage hardware. Restart attaches the database-selected release even if current's convenience copy is stale. After database commit, publication is infallible in-memory assignments and committed ephemeral rebind; fallible native compilation, declaration/environment/import validation happens before commit. Worker process spawn remains fallible after commit: failure reports committed activation with degraded worker availability and retries; it cannot undo a release or external effects. Filesystem-copy failures report the committed result.

### 5.1 Path-independent control address

The accept loop polls a nonblocking listener, but immediately resets each
accepted stream to blocking mode before peer validation or input. Darwin/BSD
inherits the listener's nonblocking flag; Linux does not. Blocking streams
let the existing total input deadline govern clients that write after accept.
If resetting the stream fails, close it and report the accept error; never
admit a command on a stream with unknown I/O mode.

Amendment commissioned and implemented by Codex for Charlie on 2026-10-12,
for the 0.4.24 macOS publication blocker. Darwin's Unix socket path limit
is 104 bytes; the previous deployment-local name failed under ordinary
`/var/folders/.../T/` fixtures. Linux's descriptor-address workaround did not
solve Darwin or provide one portable transport.

Use `/tmp/snapback4-<effective-uid>/<sha256>.sock` on both hosts. The hash is
SHA-256 over the raw bytes of the canonical **deployment directory**, binding
the socket to immutable release source. Aliases that canonicalize to the same path resolve
to one address; two deployments pointing to one external database retain
separate addresses, so only the serving deployment can submit its releases.
At deploy and serve startup, create a missing deployment privately, resolve
its canonical directory once, then use that directory for ownership and all
release paths. Serve also resolves the selected release root before constructing
the project. Worker cwd and `SNAPBACK_PROJECT_DIR` therefore report the same
canonical active release path, including Darwin's `/var` → `/private/var` and
explicit deployment symlinks. An existing deployment alias resolves before
private-directory validation; no symlink is installed inside private storage.
Use the same canonical path spelling on case/normalization-insensitive hosts;
byte-distinct canonical spellings produce distinct addresses. The request
envelope still binds the canonical database path, device and inode.
The longest address is 95 bytes (ten decimal UID digits), below both limits.
Do not use TMPDIR or XDG_RUNTIME_DIR: these may be long or differ between CLI
invocations. This deterministic address needs no durable locator file; deploy
and rollback derive exactly the same address from durable deployment location.
A moved deployment must stop and restart serve, as moving its immutable source
already requires. Socket entries are ephemeral, never backup authority.

Create the single runtime directory atomically as 0700. Open with O_DIRECTORY
and O_NOFOLLOW, inspect the opened metadata, and refuse a different UID, a
non-directory/symlink or any mode other than 0700 (including special bits).
Never repair or chmod an existing directory. `/tmp` is the platform's shared
sticky temporary root (Darwin may alias it to `/private/tmp`). An untrusted
user may pre-create a conflicting name and cause refusal, but gains no access
and causes no modification of their entry. Malicious same-UID filesystem edits
remain outside §5's threat model.

Bind only while holding the exclusive database sidecar. An existing entry
must be a same-owner 0600 socket. Probe it: a live listener refuses without
unlinking; only connection-refused/not-found permits stale replacement. Any
other error refuses. Preserve the inode check around client connect, same-UID
peer checks on both ends, envelope identity, command flock and revision CAS.
Establish inode-based cleanup custody immediately after bind, before chmod or
nonblocking setup; Drop removes only that owned socket inode. Graceful shutdown
joins control before releasing store ownership. Fatal exit after permission setup leaves a stale
socket for the next exclusive owner. A crash between bind and chmod may leave
a socket with the launching umask's mode; if it is not exactly 0600, recovery
refuses and the operator must remove that stale entry. Do not change the
process-global umask in a multithreaded host to hide this fail-closed window. Keep the shared runtime directory on exit:
removing it would race other deployments, and it contains no durable state.

Acceptance: default-TMPDIR project paths exceeding 108 bytes on Linux and
Darwin start serve, execute hot deploy and rollback without restarting, refuse
a second host, clean up on SIGTERM, and restart after SIGKILL. Unit regressions
cover stale/live socket entries, file/symlink and 0600 socket refusals, directory
permissions/type/UID checks, canonical aliases, independent deployments, and
preserving replaced entries during cleanup. SIGTERM/SIGINT/SIGHUP also drain
an incomplete control request before process exit; join the control handle
while Shared still retains the owner. An admitted activation is allowed to
finish. Enforce the five-second input bound as a total monotonic deadline,
updating the remaining socket read timeout before every read; partial input
arriving repeatedly cannot reset the deadline or prolong shutdown. The signal
regression keeps sending partial input until the host closes the connection.
No budget or public door changes.

## 6. Devices and partitions

Every changed execution activation including A→B→A gets a fresh monotone semantic generation, never reuses a release number as a generation. Execution-content identity includes the prepared native module digest as well as schema/programs. A changed native digest advances the existing semantic generation atomically with release activation, even when native declarations are unchanged; removing a module changes the digest too. Equal execution content retains generation unless a release activation changes the final active/dormant population (§9.1), which requires a new replica generation; source-only activations still advance activation revision. The digest is SHA-256 over the exact prepared wasm bytes; the no-module sentinel is the empty string persisted as release_native_digest. activation_hash binds schema/programs and that digest. Comparison is with the current activation, so A→B→A is fresh. Startup attaches the persisted execution identity and verifies the immutable release module matches its recorded digest. This uses the existing device protocol, not a separate native epoch. Rollback and forward use the same generation replacement and full partition reacquisition already used by adoption: /schema advertises the exact active shape, sync rejects old watermarks, devices clear obsolete base/index state and rebase or refuse their queued intents under the existing authority/operation contract. Store id remains unchanged; this is not a store replacement. No dormant rows or fields enter snapshots, partition logs, online answers or device stores. Never combine old-generation pages with a new backend. A late old read remains tagged old and cannot overwrite a newly observed generation. Simulate rollback/forward across a delayed sync page and audience changes; retained data changes server storage only, not device persistence formats.

Lock order: command flock is CLI-only; serving control never acquires it. Candidate filesystem/native/worker preparation precedes engine lock. Serving publication holds engine → bound → generation → native, and separately replaces readers while engine is held (matching existing checkout); no /schema handler takes engine while holding bound. Ephemeral rebind uses its existing committed infallible path, never a fallible post-commit store read. Update desired worker root before revision; worker thread releases root/bound guards before taking engine. Wake/seq/answers follow existing publish_engine while engine is held: these are short memory updates/nonblocking condition notifications, required before new writer admission; no socket/child wait occurs there. `releases` includes the existing worker status metadata so post-commit launch failure is visible as degraded availability. No SQLite lock is held while waiting for a child process or socket peer.

## 7. Implementation and acceptance

Small commits: reviewed design; transactional retention/prune/refusals; local hot-control publication; integration/device/client coverage and docs. Authoring entry: SERVER.md owns the release-lifecycle section; guide evolution owns schema interactions. OPS guide deploy gets a one-line pointer to that section.

CLI matrix: `deploy <dir> [--restore-dormant] [--allow-feed-adoption]`; `rollback <dir> [--to N] [--allow-feed-adoption]`; `prune <dir> [--allow-destructive]`; `releases <dir>`. Legacy --allow-destructive on deploy/rollback is accepted but does not prune or bypass validation, and prints the prune command when relevant. --restore-dormant authorizes only restoration, never loss. --to requires a positive existing immutable release id and may select a newer release. E_ADOPT operator text: `table.column/index: N affected rows; sample ids [...]; values/keys [...]; reason; rewrite: <bridge/repair schema or command>; inspect: snapback4 sql <query> --release <dir>`. No flag bypasses invalid values, ownership or references. Unreachable live control refuses `E_STORE_BUSY: stop snapback4 serve <dir>, rerun the exact command, then snapback4 serve <dir>` before send; after send use `releases <dir>` and explicit `rollback --to N` after inspecting the outcome, never an automatic deploy allocating N+1.

History/status is page-based with `releases <dir> [--after <event-id>]`; bounded pages return a continuation and inventory pages use stable item keys. Prune audit stores a complete item list in SQLite; bounded replies carry a continuation rather than truncate silently. Critical assertions include T(a,b) → absent T → T(a) → absent T → T(a,b), preserved b and latest updated a; identical parent id/value replacement invalidates dormant edges; stale revision after A→B→A and equal-hash activation refuses; activation-time parent expiry followed by identical-id replacement invalidates dormant references; rapid B→C activation with a barrier between B worker preparation and credential minting discards B conventional/named source; an old checked-out reader not yet pinned retries the new factory including native-only changes; a connected client holding native module A hot-adopts B with unchanged declarations, reacquires B backend/module and refuses a delayed A result; inject interruption before/after commit and during pointer-copy updates to distinguish no activation from committed activation with an ambiguous result.

Tests also cover private socket permissions/symlinks/peer credentials, racing commands and writes during validation, process crash before commit and between commit/publication/reply, live prune/status, legacy empty dormancy and unchanged device format. Tests: deploy v1/write, v2 adding table/required default/enum/index/write, precise refusal while new enum is in use, authorized repair then rollback with dormant table/column, old update/insert/delete, forward restores prior values and supplies defaults only to new rows. Also prune preview/confirmation and asset lifetime; unique failure after dormant-index writes; required default-less forward refusal; transaction failure leaves active and dormant data intact; restart binds the committed release; simultaneous hot requests and failed hot activation; stale-generation sync and real client reacquisition; simulator delayed pages/audiences. Quality bar: the commissioned core/lang/device/sim cargo suites, CLI release/serve/adoption tests, relevant client tests and ref-check. No data-loss claim rests on diagnostics alone.

## 2026-10-19 — MIG staging clock control

Classified staging stores admit serve --clock-offset and the existing clock
advance/status wire under a full service token. Production refuses offsets
before listener startup and all HTTP clock control even with operator authority.
The writer rechecks classification/session before advancing. startServe exposes
clockOffset, now and advance; it mints a current staging-clock operator session
for each control rather than caching a token across arbitrarily large jumps.
Fixture/reset/persona/job dev doors remain closed. The native-backed client
regression passed, including CLI advancement and restart dropping the offset.
The installed-package staging qualifier now exercises the same clock helpers.
LLP 3000.000.000.002 records the combined MIG language/adoption amendment;
independent review continues before final acceptance.


## 2026-10-19 — MIG declarative backfills and device bytes

Backfills are typed scalar patches evaluated before candidate narrowing, with
per-lifetime preimages and declaration membership retained through dormancy.
The existing activation transaction stages every result, checks historical
lookup uniqueness, then publishes data/indexes/derived state and generation.
Whole-table and narrowed restoration join the source snapshot. Hot adoption
and exact previews sample the serving clock, including staging offsets.

The web schema omits server-only migration metadata and evidence. Locked Linux
measurement is 2,385,285 B raw / 726,681 B gzip, +4,796 raw / +679 gzip versus
Integration 17. The small deterministic text library must use the same Rust
interpreter on server and device; the artifact allowance moves to measured
727,181 B (exactly 500 B headroom). No runtime meter ceiling changes. Family
and absent-feature audits and final qualification are recorded in the MIG
report; this measurement alone does not claim those checks have passed.

2026-10-19 MIG final wasm: metered text argument accounting measures 2,385,447 raw / 726,623 gzip bytes; budget tightened to 727,123 (measurement +500). Server-only backfill metadata remains absent from web wasm.

//! The generation barrier checks predecessor audiences before destructive projection.
// @ref LLP 3000.014#7-activation — pinned time, child scan, capture before validation, atomic publication.
use crate::{
    error::RuntimeError,
    rows::{Bounds, Dir, Rows},
    schema::{RuleExpr, Schema, Table, through::PARENT_DEADLINE},
    value::Value,
};
use std::collections::BTreeMap;

const EXPIRY_TABLE_CURSOR: &str = "$expiry-table";

/// Rotate a sweep's table order after the last table that consumed a bounded
/// visit. Per-table row cursors alone can starve every later table when an
/// earlier table contains a permanently restricted due row.
pub(crate) fn resume_expiry_tables(
    mut tables: Vec<String>,
    cursors: &BTreeMap<String, Vec<Value>>,
) -> Vec<String> {
    let after = cursors
        .get(EXPIRY_TABLE_CURSOR)
        .and_then(|cursor| cursor.first())
        .and_then(|value| value.as_str().ok());
    if let Some(index) = after.and_then(|after| tables.iter().position(|table| table == after)) {
        let next = (index + 1) % tables.len().max(1);
        tables.rotate_left(next);
    }
    tables
}

pub(crate) fn mark_expiry_table(cursors: &mut BTreeMap<String, Vec<Value>>, table: &str) {
    cursors.insert(EXPIRY_TABLE_CURSOR.into(), vec![Value::String(table.into())]);
}

/// Only inherited batches need staging. Preserve the streaming path for
/// existing schemas, including large fixture imports.
pub(crate) fn ancestor_order<'a, I>(
    schema: &Schema,
    rows: I,
) -> Box<dyn Iterator<Item = (String, Value)> + 'a>
where
    I: IntoIterator<Item = (String, Value)>,
    I::IntoIter: 'a,
{
    if schema.tables.values().all(|t| t.through.is_none()) {
        return Box::new(rows.into_iter());
    }
    let order = schema.through_order();
    let mut rows: Vec<_> = rows.into_iter().collect();
    rows.sort_by_key(|(name, _)| order.iter().position(|n| n == name).unwrap_or(usize::MAX));
    Box::new(rows.into_iter())
}

fn refusal(name: &str, count: usize, reason: &str, samples: &[String]) -> RuntimeError {
    RuntimeError::Structural {
        code: "E_READ_THROUGH_ADOPT",
        message: format!(
            "{name}: {count} child rows cannot adopt read through: {reason}; sample ids/parent values [{}]; rewrite: reconcile the named audience fields and live parents in a bridge release, then `snapback4 deploy <dir> --restore-dormant` (guide rules)",
            samples.join(", ")
        ),
    }
}

fn sample(samples: &mut Vec<String>, row: &Value, reference: &str, parent: &str) {
    fn bounded(text: &str) -> &str {
        let mut end = text.len().min(256);
        while !text.is_char_boundary(end) {
            end -= 1;
        }
        &text[..end]
    }
    if samples.len() < 5 {
        let id = row
            .field("id")
            .ok()
            .and_then(|v| v.as_str().ok())
            .unwrap_or("<missing>");
        let value = row
            .field(reference)
            .ok()
            .and_then(|v| v.as_str().ok())
            .unwrap_or("<missing>");
        samples.push(format!(
            "{}: {reference} -> {parent}[{}]",
            bounded(id),
            bounded(value)
        ));
    }
}

fn live_work(tx:&dyn Rows,name:&str,old:Option<&Table>,next:&Schema)->Result<bool,RuntimeError> {
    Ok(old.and_then(|t|t.through.as_ref()).is_some_and(|t|t.live.is_some())
        || next.table(name).and_then(|t|t.through.as_ref()).is_some_and(|t|t.live.is_some())
        || tx.has_restored_live_authority(name)?)
}

// Both definitions may coexist in a raw source image until projection.
fn parent_bytes(a:Option<&Table>,b:Option<&Table>)->u64 {
    a.into_iter().chain(b).map(crate::rows::disclosed::decode_reservation).fold(0_u64,u64::saturating_add).saturating_mul(4)
}

fn rows(
    tx: &dyn Rows, name: &str, table: &Table, parent_scratch: u64, bounded: bool, destination: Option<&Table>,
    mut visit: impl FnMut(Value) -> Result<(), RuntimeError>,
) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    if !bounded {
        let mut bounds=Bounds::default();
        loop {
            let page=tx.scan(name,"byId",&[],&bounds,Dir::Asc,256)?;
            if page.is_empty() {return Ok(());}
            for (row,key) in page {bounds.after=crate::key::decode(&key);visit(row)?;}
        }
    }
    let decode=crate::rows::disclosed::decode_reservation(table);
    let keys=crate::rows::adoption::index_scratch(name,table).saturating_add(destination.map_or(0,|t|crate::rows::adoption::index_scratch(name,t)));
    let entries=crate::rows::adoption::index_capacity(table).saturating_add(destination.map_or(0,crate::rows::adoption::index_capacity)).saturating_mul(2);
    let scratch=decode.saturating_mul(4).saturating_add(destination.map_or(0,crate::rows::disclosed::decode_reservation).saturating_mul(4)).saturating_add(keys.saturating_mul(2))
        .saturating_add(parent_scratch).saturating_add(8192);
    tx.walk_adoption(name,decode,scratch,[2,table.columns.len() as u64+16,entries],&mut visit)
}

pub(crate) fn correspondence(
    child: &RuleExpr,
    parent: &RuleExpr,
) -> Option<std::collections::BTreeMap<String, String>> {
    if child.is_uniformly_readable() && parent.is_uniformly_readable() {
        return Some(Default::default());
    }
    fn walk(
        a: &serde_json::Value,
        b: &serde_json::Value,
        map: &mut std::collections::BTreeMap<String, String>,
    ) -> bool {
        use serde_json::Value::*;
        match (a, b) {
            (Object(a), Object(b)) if a.len() == b.len() => {
                if let (Some(String(a)), Some(String(b))) = (a.get("Field"), b.get("Field")) {
                    if map.get(a).is_some_and(|old| old != b)
                        || map.iter().any(|(old, v)| v == b && old != a)
                    {
                        return false;
                    }
                    map.insert(a.clone(), b.clone());
                    true
                } else {
                    a.iter()
                        .all(|(k, a)| b.get(k).is_some_and(|b| walk(a, b, map)))
                }
            }
            (Array(a), Array(b)) if a.len() == b.len() => {
                a.iter().zip(b).all(|(a, b)| walk(a, b, map))
            }
            _ => a == b,
        }
    }
    let mut map = Default::default();
    walk(
        &serde_json::to_value(child).ok()?,
        &serde_json::to_value(parent).ok()?,
        &mut map,
    )
    .then_some(map)
}

/// The retirement phase shared by activation and its read-only occupancy
/// preview. Selection and the predecessor deadline predicate live here once;
/// checking a candidate must not count a row this phase would remove.
pub(crate) struct Retirement<'a> {
    affected: Vec<(String, &'a Table)>,
    now: i64,
}

impl<'a> Retirement<'a> {
    pub(crate) fn new(next: &Schema, previous: &'a Schema, now: i64) -> Self {
        let affected = next
            .through_order()
            .into_iter()
            .filter_map(|name| {
                let next = &next.tables[&name];
                let old = previous.table(&name)?;
                Self::changes_lifetime(next,old).then_some((name, old))
            })
            .collect();
        Self { affected, now }
    }

    fn changes_lifetime(next:&Table,old:&Table) -> bool {
        next.through != old.through || next.through.is_some() && (next.expire != old.expire || next.expire.as_ref().is_some_and(|field|old.defaults.get(field)!=next.defaults.get(field)))
    }

    pub(crate) fn retires_image(next:&Table,old:&Table,row:&Value,now:i64) -> bool {
        (next.through!=old.through || next.expire!=old.expire || old.expire.as_ref().is_some_and(|field|old.defaults.get(field)!=next.defaults.get(field))) && old.expired(row,now)
    }

    pub(crate) fn affects(&self, name: &str) -> bool {
        self.affected.iter().any(|(table, _)| table == name)
    }

    pub(crate) fn retires(&self, name: &str, row: &Value) -> bool {
        self.affected
            .iter()
            .find(|(table, _)| table == name)
            .is_some_and(|(_, old)| old.expired(row, self.now))
    }
}

/// Capture barrier for an authoritative adapter's uncommitted generation.
pub fn activate(
    tx: &dyn Rows,
    next: &Schema,
//! Preflight activation pages before owning their serialized row images.
use super::*;
use crate::{error::MeterKind, meter::{Ceilings, Cut, Meter}, rows::{adoption, word_heap as heap}};

pub(super) fn walk(tx: &Tx<'_>, table: i64, mut visit: impl FnMut(&str, Value) -> Result<(), RuntimeError>) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    walk_metered(tx, table, adoption::Work::DECODE, None, |id, row, _| visit(id, row))
}

// @ref LLP 3000.007#20.24.1 — reserve stored bytes before fetching or decoding; page scratch stays live until consumed.
pub(super) fn walk_metered(tx: &Tx<'_>, table: i64, work: adoption::Work, scratch: Option<&dyn Fn(&Value) -> crate::value::InternalPrice>,
    visit: impl FnMut(&str, Value, &mut Meter) -> Result<(), RuntimeError>) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    let checked=|row:&Value|Ok(scratch.map_or_else(crate::value::InternalPrice::zero,|f|f(row)));
    walk_metered_checked(tx,table,work,scratch.map(|_|&checked as &dyn Fn(&Value)->Result<crate::value::InternalPrice,RuntimeError>),visit)
}

/// Row-specific provenance can fail closed before any page callback runs.
pub(super) fn walk_metered_checked(tx: &Tx<'_>, table: i64, work: adoption::Work,
    scratch: Option<&dyn Fn(&Value)->Result<crate::value::InternalPrice,RuntimeError>>,
    mut visit: impl FnMut(&str, Value, &mut Meter) -> Result<(), RuntimeError>) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    const PAGE: usize = 256;
    let mut after: Option<String> = None;
    loop {
        // One job meter per page, shared with contribution evaluation. Retain
        // the continuation's allowance across the page; reset only after all
        // its row images and callback scratch have been consumed and dropped.
        let mut meter = Meter::new(Ceilings::job(), Cut::Abort);
        meter.at("adopt stored rows");
        meter.heap(after.as_deref().map_or(0, heap::text))?;
        meter.once(crate::meter::HeapSite::SqliteColumns, 2 * tx.page_size)?;
        let page_mark = meter.mark();
        let mut limit = PAGE;
        let mut page = loop {
            limit = work.limit(&meter, limit);
            let sizes = if after.is_some() {
                "SELECT octet_length(id), octet_length(data) FROM r WHERE t = ?1 AND id > ?2 ORDER BY id LIMIT ?3"
            } else {
                "SELECT octet_length(id), octet_length(data) FROM r WHERE t = ?1 AND ?2 IS NULL ORDER BY id LIMIT ?3"
            };
            let (mut count, mut bytes) = (0, 0u64);
            {
                let mut statement = tx.tx.prepare_cached(sizes)?;
                let mut rows = statement.query(params![table, after, limit as i64])?;
                while let Some(row) = rows.next()? {
                    let id_bytes = row.get::<_, i64>(0)? as usize;
                    let data_bytes = row.get::<_, i64>(1)? as usize;
                    // Rust-owned text, two native payload copies, allocation
                    // headers and exact Vec slots. Length preflight borrows no
                    // overflow payload (the same SQLite convention as scans).
                    let next = bytes.saturating_add(3 * (heap::allocation(id_bytes) + heap::allocation(data_bytes)) + 64 + heap::slots::<(String, String)>(1));
                    // This first size estimate bounds the fetch; admission below
                    // also checks the actual decoder and contribution allowance.
                    if count > 0 && next > meter.ceilings.heap_bytes / 2 { break; }
                    meter.heap(next - bytes)?;
                    bytes = next;
                    count += 1;
                }
            }
            if count == 0 { return Ok(()); }
            let query = if after.is_some() {
                "SELECT id, data FROM r WHERE t = ?1 AND id > ?2 ORDER BY id LIMIT ?3"
            } else {
                "SELECT id, data FROM r WHERE t = ?1 AND ?2 IS NULL ORDER BY id LIMIT ?3"
            };
            let mut page = Vec::<(String, String)>::with_capacity(count);
            {
                let mut statement = tx.tx.prepare_cached(query)?;
                let mut rows = statement.query(params![table, after, count as i64])?;
                while let Some(row) = rows.next()? { page.push((row.get(0)?, row.get(1)?)); }
            }
            if adoption::page_fits_checked(page.iter().map(|(_, data)| data.as_str()), &mut meter, work, scratch)? { break page; }
            limit = (page.len() / 2).max(1);
            drop(page);
            meter.release_heap(page_mark.clone());
        };
        for (id, data) in &page {
            let row_mark = meter.mark();
            meter.charge(MeterKind::Examined, 1)?;
            // Decode plus copy-on-write projection, optional materialization
            // and validation scratch. Contribution keys reserve more through
            // this same meter, on top of the still-resident complete page.
            adoption::charge(&mut meter, adoption::decode_bytes(data))?;
            let row = serde_json::from_str(data).map_err(|e| RuntimeError::Store(e.to_string()))?;
            visit(id, row, &mut meter)?;
            meter.release_heap(row_mark);
        }
        // Move the last ID into the next page's continuation, without an
        // unpriced clone. Drop every other fetched byte before releasing.
        after = page.pop().map(|row| row.0);
        drop(page);
        meter.release_heap(page_mark);
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    fn fixture(count: usize, payload: usize) -> Store {
        let mut store = Store::memory().unwrap();
        let tx = store.transaction().unwrap();
        for n in 0..count {
            let id = format!("r{n:04}");
            let data = canonical_json(&crate::value::object([
                ("id", Value::Id(id.clone())), ("payload", Value::String("x".repeat(payload))),
            ]));
            tx.tx.execute("INSERT INTO r(t,id,data) VALUES(1,?1,?2)", params![id, data]).unwrap();
        }
        tx.commit().unwrap();
        store
    }

    #[test]
    fn callback_keeps_the_whole_fetched_page_reserved() {
        let mut store = Store::memory().unwrap();
        let tx = store.transaction().unwrap();
        let mut serialized_bytes = 0;
        for n in 0..20 {
            let id = format!("r{n:04}");
            let data = canonical_json(&crate::value::object([
                ("id", Value::Id(id.clone())), ("payload", Value::String("x".repeat(1024))),
            ]));
            serialized_bytes += (id.len() + data.len()) as u64;
            tx.tx.execute("INSERT INTO r(t,id,data) VALUES(1,?1,?2)", params![id, data]).unwrap();
        }
        let mut calls = 0;
        let mut first_heap = None;
        walk_metered(&tx, 1, adoption::Work::DECODE, None, |_, row, meter| {
            let mut decoder = Meter::new(Ceilings::unbounded(), Cut::Abort);
            adoption::charge(&mut decoder, adoption::decode_bytes(&canonical_json(&row)))?;
            let decode = decoder.used.heap_bytes;
            // Independent lower bound: Rust-owned images and both native
            // payload copies coexist with decode. This excludes headers/slots,
            // so it does not just repeat the walker's allocation formula.
            assert!(meter.used.heap_bytes >= 3 * serialized_bytes + decode,
                "Rust and native page copies must coexist with decoder scratch: {:?}", meter.used);
            let expected = first_heap.get_or_insert(meter.used.heap_bytes);
            assert_eq!(meter.used.heap_bytes, *expected, "earlier rows release only their own scratch");
            assert_eq!(meter.used.examined, 20 + calls + 1, "all admission decodes plus callback decodes are charged");
            calls += 1;
            Ok(())
        }).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(calls, 20);
        tx.rollback().unwrap();
    }

    #[test]
    fn heap_retries_keep_admission_examinations_at_the_callback_boundary() {
        let mut store = fixture(5, 1280 << 10);
        let tx = store.transaction().unwrap();
        let work = adoption::Work { examined: 2, steps: 1, index_entries: 1 };
        let scratch = |row: &Value| adoption::contribution_bytes(row, &crate::schema::public_table(Default::default(), Default::default()));
        let mut observed = Vec::new();
        walk_metered(&tx, 1, work, Some(&scratch), |_, row, meter| {
            observed.push(meter.used.examined);
            assert_eq!((meter.used.steps, meter.used.index_entries), (0, 0), "rejected attempts perform no contribution work");
            meter.charge(MeterKind::Examined, 1)?;
            meter.step(1)?;
            adoption::charge(meter, scratch(&row))?;
            meter.charge(MeterKind::IndexEntries, 1)?;
            assert!(meter.heap_peak <= Ceilings::job().heap_bytes);
            Ok(())
        }).unwrap();
        // Four two-image attempts each decode one row before the heap check
        // asks for a singleton. Its admission and callback decode add two.
        // The last source is already a singleton and needs no rejected attempt.
        assert_eq!(observed, [3, 3, 3, 3, 2]);
        tx.rollback().unwrap();
    }

    #[test]
    fn every_cumulative_budget_fixes_the_page_before_any_callback() {
        for (work, sizes) in [
            (adoption::Work { examined: 64, steps: 63, index_entries: 63 }, vec![252, 4]),
            (adoption::Work { examined: 2, steps: 5040, index_entries: 1 }, vec![198, 58]),
            (adoption::Work { examined: 1, steps: 0, index_entries: 400 }, vec![40, 40, 40, 40, 40, 40, 16]),
        ] {
            let mut store = fixture(256, 0);
            let tx = store.transaction().unwrap();
            let (mut page, mut offset, mut calls) = (0, 0u64, 0);
            walk_metered(&tx, 1, work, None, |_, _, meter| {
                assert_eq!(meter.used.examined, sizes[page] + offset * work.examined + 1,
                    "the entire admission pass and every earlier callback remain charged");
                assert_eq!(meter.used.steps, offset * work.steps);
                assert_eq!(meter.used.index_entries, offset * work.index_entries);
                meter.charge(MeterKind::Examined, work.examined - 1)?;
                meter.step(work.steps)?;
                meter.charge(MeterKind::IndexEntries, work.index_entries)?;
                calls += 1;
                offset += 1;
                if offset == sizes[page] { page += 1; offset = 0; }
                Ok(())
            }).unwrap();
            assert_eq!(calls, 256);
            assert_eq!(page, sizes.len());
            tx.rollback().unwrap();
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn only_non_fitting_singletons_refuse_before_callbacks() {
        for (work, kind) in [
            (adoption::Work { examined: 16_384, steps: 0, index_entries: 0 }, MeterKind::Examined),
            (adoption::Work { examined: 1, steps: 1_000_001, index_entries: 0 }, MeterKind::Steps),
            (adoption::Work { examined: 1, steps: 0, index_entries: 16_385 }, MeterKind::IndexEntries),
        ] {
            for count in [0, 1] {
                let mut store = fixture(count, 0);
                let tx = store.transaction().unwrap();
                let mut calls = 0;
                let result = walk_metered(&tx, 1, work, None, |_, _, _| { calls += 1; Ok(()) });
                assert_eq!(calls, 0, "no callback can start with an impossible page budget");
                if count == 0 { result.unwrap(); }
                else { assert!(matches!(result, Err(RuntimeError::Bound { kind: got, .. }) if got == kind), "{result:?}"); }
                tx.rollback().unwrap();
            }
        }
    }
}

   213	// Prune forgets historical values, retaining only the fact that exact reversal
   214	// is no longer available. No tombstone can be queried by an app.
   215	pub(super) fn prune(tx:&Tx<'_>)->Result<(),RuntimeError>{
   216	    if present(tx)? {tx.conn().execute_batch("DELETE FROM _backfill_evidence WHERE NOT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM r WHERE r.t=_backfill_evidence.t AND r.id=_backfill_evidence.id); DELETE FROM _backfill_pruned WHERE NOT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM r WHERE r.t=_backfill_pruned.t AND r.id=_backfill_pruned.id); INSERT OR IGNORE INTO _backfill_pruned SELECT t,id,definition,active FROM _backfill_evidence; DELETE FROM _backfill_evidence;")?;}
        self.unsafe_dev_doors || !(bound.schema.auth.password && !bound.schema.auth.guests)
    }
    // Revalidate under the writer: neither a slow body nor an adoption queue
    // may carry fixture authority into a newly password-only schema.
    pub(crate) fn dev_authority(&self, engine: &Engine) -> Result<(), Refusal> {
        if self.personas && (self.unsafe_dev_doors || !(engine.schema().auth.password && !engine.schema().auth.guests)) { Ok(()) }
        else { Err(dev_doors::closed()) }
    }
    pub fn now(&self) -> i64 { lock(&self.clock).now().max(self.time_audience_clock.load(Ordering::Acquire)) }
    pub(crate) fn refresh_time_audience_clock(&self, engine: &Engine, now: i64) {
        if engine.schema().tables.values().any(|table| table.not_before.is_some()) {
            let durable = engine.store().meta("time_audience_clock").ok().flatten()
                .and_then(|value| value.parse::<i64>().ok()).unwrap_or(i64::MIN);
            self.time_audience_clock.fetch_max(now.max(durable), Ordering::AcqRel);
        } else {
            self.time_audience_clock.store(i64::MIN, Ordering::Release);
        }
    }
    fn advance_time_audiences(&self) -> Result<i64, snapback4_core::RuntimeError> {
        let requested = lock(&self.clock).now();
        // Non-timed reader traffic must retain its independent reader lane,
        // including while adoption or a mutation deliberately holds the writer.
        if self.time_audience_clock.load(Ordering::Acquire) == i64::MIN { return Ok(requested); }
        let mut engine = lock(&self.engine);
        let before = engine.seq()?;
        let now = engine.advance_time_audiences_at(requested)?;
        self.refresh_time_audience_clock(&engine, now);
        if engine.seq()? != before { self.durable_committed(&engine); }
        Ok(now)
    }
    fn next_time_audience(&self) -> Result<Option<i64>, snapback4_core::RuntimeError> {
        if self.time_audience_clock.load(Ordering::Acquire) == i64::MIN { return Ok(None); }
        lock(&self.engine).next_time_audience_at()
    }
    fn time_audience_delay(&self, at: i64) -> Duration {
        let raw = lock(&self.clock).now();
        let floor = self.time_audience_clock.load(Ordering::Acquire);
        Duration::from_millis(if at <= floor { 0 } else { at.saturating_sub(raw).max(0) }.try_into().unwrap_or(u64::MAX))
    }
    pub(crate) fn worker_owner(&self) -> Option<Arc<std::fs::File>> { self.owner.as_ref().map(|owner| owner.worker_lock()) }
}
