//! The binder's type model: what a Quarry expression is, statically, so a
//! program's arguments and result reach the client as TypeScript types by
//! inference (nothing is annotated, nothing is committed).

use crate::ast::{ScalarType, TypeKind};
use snapback4_core::ir::ArgType;
use snapback4_core::schema::{ColumnType, TextFormat};
use std::collections::BTreeMap;

#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum Ty {
    Unknown,
    Null,
    Bool,
    Int,
    Time,
    Decimal(u8),
    Money(String, u8),
    Text(u32),
    Id,
    Principal,
    Ref(String),
    Bytes,
    Json,
    Image,
    Video,
    File,
    Enum(Vec<String>),
    Cursor,
    Optional(Box<Ty>),
    /// A bounded list of scalars; `max` bounds a loop over it.
    List(Box<Ty>, u32),
    /// One row of a table, present.
    Row(String),
    WriteRow(String),
    /// A bounded page of rows, in index order.
    Page(String, u32),
    /// A shaped page: at most `max` objects.
    Collection(Box<Ty>, u32),
    Object(BTreeMap<String, Ty>),
    /// A projection whose row base carries optional device metadata.
    RowShape(BTreeMap<String, Ty>),
    /// Object-spread fields with row metadata, but ordinary object wildcard order.
    RowObject(BTreeMap<String, Ty>),

}

impl Ty {
    /// A reassigned allocation is bounded by the heap meter, not its first literal's length.
    pub(crate) fn rebound(self) -> Ty {
        match self {
            Ty::Text(_) => Ty::Text(u32::MAX),
            // A known empty value stays empty, even at a loop header.
            Ty::List(_, 0) | Ty::Collection(_, 0) => self,
            Ty::List(item, _) => Ty::List(Box::new(item.rebound()), u32::MAX),
            Ty::Collection(item, _) => Ty::Collection(Box::new(item.rebound()), u32::MAX),
            Ty::Object(fields) => Ty::Object(fields.into_iter().map(|(name, ty)| (name, ty.rebound())).collect()),
            Ty::RowShape(fields) => Ty::RowShape(fields.into_iter().map(|(name, ty)| (name, ty.rebound())).collect()),
            Ty::RowObject(fields) => Ty::RowObject(fields.into_iter().map(|(name, ty)| (name, ty.rebound())).collect()),
            Ty::Optional(inner) => inner.rebound().optional(),
            other => other,
        }
    }

    pub fn optional(self) -> Ty {
        match self {
            Ty::Optional(_) | Ty::Null | Ty::Unknown => self,
            other => Ty::Optional(Box::new(other)),
        }
    }

    pub fn required(&self) -> Ty {
        match self {
            Ty::Optional(inner) => inner.as_ref().clone(),
            other => other.clone(),
        }
    }

    pub fn is_optional(&self) -> bool {
        matches!(self, Ty::Optional(_) | Ty::Null)
    }

    pub fn is_bool(&self) -> bool {
        matches!(self, Ty::Bool)
    }

    pub fn is_numeric(&self) -> bool {
        matches!(self, Ty::Int | Ty::Time | Ty::Decimal(_) | Ty::Money(..))
    }

//! The schema: tables, columns, indexes, rules, constraints, maintained
//! aggregates, and the two words Snapback 4 adds to a table — `sync`, the
//! partition declaration (LLP 2000.000 §7.1), and the leave policy that
//! survives from Snapback 2's `retain` (LLP 2000.000 §7.2).
//!
//! The serialized form is Snapback 2's, so the conformance corpus under
//! `corpus/` loads unchanged; fields Snapback 4 deleted (`deliver`,
//! `tombstones`, `channels`) are ignored on the way in.

use crate::ir::{ArgType, KeyProvenance};
use crate::value::{Value, digest};
use serde::{Deserialize, Serialize};
use std::collections::{BTreeMap, BTreeSet};
use std::fmt;

#[cfg(feature = "sqlite")]
pub mod backfill;
pub mod columns;
pub mod reservations;
pub mod through;
pub mod live;
pub(crate) mod time_audience;
pub mod references;
mod reference;
mod update_images;
pub mod ephemeral;
mod work;
pub use work::{ActionSpec, WorkPolicy, WorkSpec};
pub mod routes;
pub mod timeline;
mod capabilities;
mod union;
mod schedule;
pub use schedule::{CALENDAR_MONTHS_LIMIT, CALENDAR_OFFSET_LIMIT_MS};
mod delivery;
mod receipts;
pub use receipts::ReceiptFamily;
pub use delivery::DeliveryBinding;
pub mod field_authority;
pub mod own_keys;
pub use field_authority::FieldAuthority;
pub use routes::RouteSpec;
#[derive(Clone, Debug, Default, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct Schema {
    #[cfg(feature = "sqlite")]
    pub backfills: Vec<backfill::Backfill>,
    pub receipts: Vec<ReceiptFamily>,
    /// Schema-time closure; retained on device projections without private derivations.
    pub receipt_dependencies: BTreeMap<String, BTreeSet<String>>,
    /// Public, schema-derived reservation prices; private maintained formulas
    /// are omitted from device projections, but their write charge is not.
    pub source_reservations: BTreeMap<String, reservations::WriteReservation>,
    /// Decode-only compatibility fence; programs narrow it before admission.
    pub legacy_receipt_prediction: bool,
    /// Capabilities inferred from old persisted schema bytes rather than
    /// declared by their producer. Servers may adopt and restamp those bytes;
    /// devices must not execute them under newer semantics.
    #[doc(hidden)]
    pub legacy_semantic_capabilities: LegacySemanticCapabilities,
    pub tables: BTreeMap<String, Table>,
    /// Nonsemantic inspection input; only the catalog side record persists it.
    /// Never part of schema equality, hashes, generation bytes or device input.
    #[cfg(feature = "sqlite")]
    pub authored_indexes: inspection::AuthoredIndexes,
    pub delivery: Vec<DeliveryBinding>,
    #[cfg(feature = "sqlite")]
    pub delivery_lookup: crate::engine::delivery::Lookup,
    pub timelines: Vec<timeline::Timeline>,
    pub maintains: Vec<Maintain>,
    pub auth: Auth,
    pub effects: Vec<String>,
    pub schedules: Vec<Schedule>,
    /// Compiled execution facts; absent on serialized conformance inputs.
    pub operations: BTreeMap<String, Execution>,
    /// Author refusals keyed by table and unique index; changing wording is not a physical index migration.
    pub unique_refusals: BTreeMap<String, BTreeMap<String, UniqueRefusal>>,
    /// Server work contracts; kept outside the interpreter/corpus IR.
    pub action_specs: BTreeMap<String, ActionSpec>,
    pub effect_specs: BTreeMap<String, WorkSpec>,
    pub routes: BTreeMap<String, RouteSpec>,
}

/// Missing optional columns read as null; missing required columns with a
/// literal default read that value. Contextual defaults belong to inserts.
pub fn materialize_optional_fields(row: &mut Value, table: &Table) {
    let fields = match row {
        Value::Row(fields) | Value::Map(fields) => fields,
        _ => return,
    };
    for (name, kind) in &table.columns {
        if !fields.contains_key(name) {
            if table.maintained.contains_key(name) {
                fields.insert(name.clone(), Value::Int(0));
            } else if kind.is_optional() {
                fields.insert(name.clone(), Value::Null);
            } else if let Some(ColumnDefault::Literal(value)) = table.defaults.get(name) {
                fields.insert(name.clone(), value.clone());
            }
        }
    }
}

/// Borrow complete images; materialize old images only when needed.
pub fn row_image<'a>(row: &'a Value, table: &Table) -> std::borrow::Cow<'a, Value> {
    if table.columns.keys().all(|column| row.field(column).is_ok()) { return std::borrow::Cow::Borrowed(row); }
    let mut image = row.clone();
    materialize_optional_fields(&mut image, table);
    std::borrow::Cow::Owned(image)
}

/// The type an argument declares as a column type, for value validation.
pub fn arg_as_column(kind: &ArgType) -> Option<ColumnType> {
    Some(match kind {
        ArgType::Cursor | ArgType::Scalar => return None,
        ArgType::Id => ColumnType::Id,
        ArgType::Principal => ColumnType::Principal,
        ArgType::Ref { table } => ColumnType::Ref { table: table.clone() },
        ArgType::Text { max, .. } => ColumnType::Text { max: *max },
        ArgType::Bytes { max } => ColumnType::Bytes { max: *max },
        ArgType::Image { max } => ColumnType::Image { max: *max, once: false },
        ArgType::Video { max } => ColumnType::Video { max: *max },
        ArgType::File { max, types } => ColumnType::File { max: *max, types: types.clone() },
        ArgType::Int => ColumnType::Int,
        ArgType::IntRange { min, max } => ColumnType::IntRange { min: *min, max: *max },
        ArgType::Bool => ColumnType::Bool,
        ArgType::Time => ColumnType::Time,
        ArgType::Json { max_bytes } => ColumnType::Json { max_bytes: *max_bytes },
        ArgType::Optional(inner) => ColumnType::Optional(Box::new(arg_as_column(inner)?)),
        ArgType::List { of, max } => ColumnType::List { of: Box::new(of.as_deref().map(arg_as_column).flatten().unwrap_or(ColumnType::Json { max_bytes: 4096 })), max: *max },
        ArgType::ListRange { of, min, max } => ColumnType::ListRange { of: Box::new(of.as_deref().and_then(arg_as_column).unwrap_or(ColumnType::Json { max_bytes: 4096 })), min: *min, max: *max },
        let name: String =
            tx.conn()
                .query_row("SELECT name FROM _catalog WHERE num=?1", [t], |r| r.get(0))?;
        let Some(table) = next
            .table(&name)
            .filter(|t| t.ephemeral.is_none() && next.maintain_for(&name).is_none())
        else {
            continue;
        };
        let old: Table =

snapback4/crates/snapback4-core/src/rows.rs:159:pub fn index_keys(index: &Index, row: &Value, id: &str) -> Vec<Vec<u8>> {
// @ref LLP 3000.007.003#3.2 — attribution is stored on the contributing row, never resolved by the aggregate evaluator.
#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub struct RecordedField {
    pub reference: String,
    pub table: String,
    pub column: String,
}

#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub enum ColumnDefault {
    Literal(Value),
    Now,
    Viewer,
}

impl ColumnDefault {
    pub fn value(&self, now: i64, viewer: &str) -> Value {
        match self {
            Self::Literal(value) => value.clone(),
            Self::Now => Value::Int(now),
            Self::Viewer => Value::Id(viewer.to_owned()),
        }
    }
}

impl Table {
    pub fn index(&self, name: &str) -> Option<&Index> {
        self.indexes.get(name)
    }

    pub fn cap(&self, by: &[String]) -> Option<u32> {
        self.constraints.iter().find_map(|constraint| match constraint {
            Constraint::Cap { by: group, max } if group == by && !self.cap_exempts_null(group) => Some(*max),
            _ => None,
        })
    }

    /// A cap grouping by a `set null` reference does not count its null rows
    /// (LLP 3000.039 §2.2), so it proves no population bound.
    pub fn cap_exempts_null(&self, by: &[String]) -> bool {
        by.iter().any(|column| self.on_delete.get(column) == Some(&references::OnDelete::SetNull))
    }

    pub fn immutable_fields(&self) -> impl Iterator<Item = &str> {
        self.constraints.iter().flat_map(|constraint| match constraint {
            Constraint::Immutable { fields } => fields.iter().map(String::as_str).collect::<Vec<_>>(),
            _ => Vec::new(),
        })
    }

    /// Whether `row` is past its `expire` column at `now`: absent to every
    /// reader, pending the sweep. A null time never expires.
    pub fn expired(&self, row: &Value, now: i64) -> bool {
        self.deadline(row).is_some_and(|at| at <= now)
    }

    /// The index the expiry sweep walks: the first leading with the
    /// `expire` column, as the declaration requires.
    pub fn expiry_index(&self) -> Option<(&str, &Index)> {
        let column = self.expiry_column()?;
        self.indexes.iter().find(|(_, index)| {
                index.is_plain() && index.columns().first().map(String::as_str) == Some(column)
            }).map(|(name, index)| (name.as_str(), index))
    }

    /// The column carrying the principal a `sync to T[...]` audience reads.
    pub fn principal_columns(&self) -> Vec<&str> {
        self.columns.iter().filter(|(_, kind)| matches!(kind, ColumnType::Principal)).map(|(name, _)| name.as_str()).collect()
    }
}

impl Tx<'_> {
    pub(super) fn clear_activation_scan(&self) {
        if let Some(buffer) = &self.activation_scan {
            buffer.borrow_mut().take();
        }
    }

    /// None means use the ordinary adapter; Some(None) proves an empty range.
    /// No Value is decoded by read-ahead, so malformed/unvisited payloads do not
    /// change a caller's result or refusal. A too-wide first row uses the old path.
    pub(super) fn activation_head(
        &self,
        t: i64,
        ti: i64,
        lo: &[u8],
        hi: &[u8],
    ) -> Result<Option<Option<(String, Vec<u8>)>>, RuntimeError> {
        let Some(buffer) = &self.activation_scan else {
            return Ok(None);
        };
        let mut held = buffer.borrow_mut();
        if let Some(page) = held.as_mut() {
            if page.table == t && page.index == ti && page.hi == hi && lo >= page.lo.as_slice() {
                while page
                    .rows
                    .front()
                    .is_some_and(|(_, key)| key.as_slice() < lo)
                {
                    page.rows.pop_front();
                }
                if let Some(row) = page.rows.pop_front() {
                    // Only forward cursors may reuse the unread tail.
                    page.lo = crate::key::upper_bound(&row.1);
                    return Ok(Some(Some(row)));
                }
            }
        }
        held.take();
        const PAGE: usize = 32;
        const BYTES: u64 = 128 * 1024;
        let mut meter = Meter::new(
            Ceilings {
                heap_bytes: BYTES,
                ..Ceilings::job()
            },
            Cut::Abort,
        );
        meter.at("adopt serialized read-ahead");
        let fixed = heap::slots::<(String, Vec<u8>)>(PAGE)
            + heap::allocation(lo.len())
            + heap::allocation(hi.len())
            // upper_bound clones then appends: reserve the old cursor, clone,
            // and the Vec growth replacement together, including capacity.
            + 4 * heap::allocation(2 * crate::rows::MAX_INDEX_KEY_BYTES + 1)
            + 2 * self.page_size;
        // An unusually large SQLite page can simply disable this optimization.
        if !meter.room(crate::error::MeterKind::HeapBytes, fixed) {
            return Ok(None);
        }
        meter.heap(fixed)?;
        let mut count = 0;
        {
            let mut statement = self.tx.prepare_cached("SELECT octet_length(r.data), length(i.k) FROM i JOIN r ON r.t = ?1 AND r.id = i.id WHERE i.ti = ?2 AND i.k >= ?3 AND i.k < ?4 ORDER BY i.k LIMIT 32")?;
            let mut rows = statement.query(params![t, ti, lo, hi])?;
            while let Some(row) = rows.next()? {
                let bytes = 3
                    * (heap::allocation(row.get::<_, i64>(0)? as usize)
                        + heap::allocation(row.get::<_, i64>(1)? as usize))
                    + 64;
                if !meter.room(crate::error::MeterKind::HeapBytes, bytes) {
                    break;
                }
                meter.heap(bytes)?;
                meter.charge(crate::error::MeterKind::Examined, 1)?;
                count += 1;
            }
        }
        if count == 0 {
            return Ok(None);
        }
        let mut page = Page {
            table: t,
            index: ti,
            lo: lo.to_vec(),
            hi: hi.to_vec(),
            rows: VecDeque::with_capacity(PAGE),
            _meter: meter,
        };
        {
            let mut statement = self.tx.prepare_cached(scan_sql(Dir::Asc))?;
            let mut rows = statement.query(params![t, ti, lo, hi, count])?;
            while let Some(row) = rows.next()? {
                page.rows.push_back((row.get(0)?, row.get(1)?));
            }
        }
        let first = page.rows.pop_front();
        if let Some((_, key)) = &first {
            page.lo = crate::key::upper_bound(key);
        }
        *held = Some(page);
        Ok(Some(first))
    }
}
    /// amplification the per-recipient log costs.
    pub fn sizes(&self) -> Result<Sizes, RuntimeError> {
        self.check_ownership()?;
        let sum = |sql: &str| -> Result<u64, RuntimeError> { Ok(self.conn.query_row(sql, [], |row| row.get::<_, Option<i64>>(0))?.unwrap_or(0).max(0) as u64) };
        // Read-only inspection also works before the next writer migrates _ops.
        let columns: Vec<String> = self.conn.prepare("SELECT name FROM pragma_table_info('_ops')")?
            .query_map([], |row| row.get(0))?.collect::<Result<_, _>>()?;
        let binding_sizes = ["name", "args_digest", "at"].into_iter().filter(|name| columns.iter().any(|column| column == name))
            .map(|name| format!(" + COALESCE(LENGTH({name}), 0)")).collect::<String>();
        Ok(Sizes {
            rows: sum("SELECT SUM(LENGTH(data) + LENGTH(id)) FROM r")?,
            row_count: sum("SELECT COUNT(*) FROM r")?,
            indexes: sum("SELECT SUM(LENGTH(k) + LENGTH(id)) FROM i")?,
            log: sum("SELECT SUM(COALESCE(LENGTH(data), 0) + LENGTH(id) + LENGTH(partition) + LENGTH(t)) FROM _log")?,
            log_events: sum("SELECT COUNT(*) FROM _log")?,
            ops: sum(&format!("SELECT SUM(LENGTH(outcome) + LENGTH(id) + LENGTH(principal) + LENGTH(seq){binding_sizes}) FROM _ops"))?,
            file: sum("SELECT page_count * page_size FROM pragma_page_count(), pragma_page_size()")?,
        })
    }
}

#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, Default, PartialEq, Eq, serde::Serialize)]
pub struct Sizes {
    pub rows: u64,
    pub row_count: u64,
    pub indexes: u64,
    pub log: u64,
    pub log_events: u64,
    pub ops: u64,
    pub file: u64,
}
//! A bounded serialized read-ahead buffer used only inside server activation.
//! Logical callers still fetch and price exactly one row at a time.
// @ref LLP 3000.002#implementation-follow-up — separate bounded raw workspace preserves logical admission.
use super::*;
use crate::{
    meter::{Ceilings, Cut, Meter},
    rows::word_heap as heap,
};
use std::{cell::RefCell, collections::VecDeque};

pub(super) type Buffer = RefCell<Option<Page>>;

pub(super) struct Page {
    table: i64,
    index: i64,
    lo: Vec<u8>,
    hi: Vec<u8>,
    rows: VecDeque<(String, Vec<u8>)>,
    /// Retain the admission for the complete buffer, including consumed slots.
    _meter: Meter,
}

        tx.commit().unwrap();

        let private_page = !visibility.uniform && !always_visible;
        let mut page_examined = private_page;
        let mut walk_remaining = AUTHORIZATION_WALK_LIMIT;
        if private_page {
            self.authorized_scan(&[
                (MeterKind::Examined, u64::from(!aborting)),
                (MeterKind::RuleProbes, rules::rule_probes(&table.rules.read).saturating_mul(take.saturating_add(1) as u64),
                    ),
            ], |_, _| Ok(()))?;
            if self.read_capped() { return Ok((Vec::new(), Vec::new())); }
        }
        let horizon = table.sync.as_ref().and_then(|sync| sync.horizon.as_ref());
        // Window boundaries certify only single-audience descending scans.
        let by_boundary = self.device && !scan.existence && horizon.is_some() && table.sync.as_ref().is_some_and(|sync| sync.single_window(&scan.index).is_some()) && dir == Dir::Desc;
        let boundary = if by_boundary { self.tx.acquired_boundary(&scan.table, &scan.index, &prefix)? } else { None };
        let mut crossed = false;
        let mut candidate_inside = false;
        // Internal timeline scans retain complete source rows, including arbitrary
        // application payload. Use the same pre-allocation ledger as word scans.
        // Ordinary jobs may hold an empty Purge grant; that is not a transition.
        let metered_payload = word.is_some_and(|w| w.metered) || self.view_scan_active
            || matches!(&self.timeline_authority, Some(super::timeline_authority::Authority::Transition(_)));
        // @ref LLP 3000.015#1 — reserve declared decode scratch, never actual private bytes.
        // Both adapters use the typed projection boundary before retention.
        let private_payload = table.written_by.values().any(|a| !a.audience);
        let streaming = aggregate.is_some();
        let transient = streaming || metered_domain;
        #[cfg(feature = "server-search")]
        let prepaid_head = !self.device && self.query_program && matches!(aggregate.as_deref(), Some(super::extrema::Aggregate::Keys { .. } | super::extrema::Aggregate::Head { .. }));
        // @ref LLP 3000.027#2 — a declared cursorless query needs no overflow row;
        // cursor positions, ordered merges, lane discovery and horizons still do.
        let stop_at_bound = !aborting && !scan.opaque_cursor && scan.merge_site.is_none() && word.is_none()
            && !self.discovering_values && !self.view_scan_active && !(self.device && horizon.is_some())
            && !transient && !job_page && scan.take != Bound::Meter;
        if metered_payload || transient { self.meter.heap(DisclosedBounds(&bounds).heap())?; }
        let mut cursor = bounds.clone();

        // Key payload sizes vary: only grow this reservation when a
        // larger key is encountered, before its decode or clone can allocate.
        let mut cursor_heap = 0;
        let batch = if transient || job_page { 1 } else if visibility.uniform || granted { take + 1 } else { (take + 1).max(32) };
        let mut row_heap = None;
        let mut row_bytes = 0;
        let mut consumed = 0;

        'outer: loop {
            if stop_at_bound && visible.len() == take { break; }
            let batch = if stop_at_bound { batch.min(take - visible.len()) } else { batch };

                if let Some(mark) = row_heap.take() { self.meter.release_row_heap(mark, row_bytes); }

                self.read_charge(MeterKind::Examined, 1)?;
                capped = true;

            // No adaptive lookahead, even for non-uniform rules. A later chunk
            // establishes exhaustion; every new candidate must first fit.

impl Sizes {
    /// The log's bytes per byte of rows.
    pub fn amplification(&self) -> f64 {
        if self.rows == 0 { 0.0 } else { self.log as f64 / self.rows as f64 }
    }
}

impl<'a> Tx<'a> {
    pub fn conn(&self) -> &Connection {
        self.clear_activation_scan();
        &self.tx
    }

    pub fn commit(self) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        self.tx.commit()?;
        Ok(())
    }

    pub fn rollback(self) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        self.tx.rollback()?;
        Ok(())
    }

    /// A savepoint, so a program can roll back its own writes and keep the
    /// enclosing bookkeeping (a refused mutation still records its outcome).
    pub fn savepoint(&self, name: &str) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        self.conn().execute_batch(&format!("SAVEPOINT {name}"))?;
        Ok(())
    }

    pub fn release(&self, name: &str) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        self.conn().execute_batch(&format!("RELEASE {name}"))?;
        Ok(())
    }

    /// Roll back the named savepoint and release it in the same operation.
    pub fn rollback_to(&self, name: &str) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        self.clear_activation_scan();
        self.changes.borrow_mut().widen();
        self.conn().execute_batch(&format!("ROLLBACK TO {name}; RELEASE {name}"))?;
        // Lazy sequence-table creation may have belonged to this savepoint.
        self.sequence_table.set(None);
        Ok(())
    }

    fn num(&self, name: &str) -> Result<i64, RuntimeError> {
        self.catalog.get(name).copied().ok_or_else(|| RuntimeError::Schema(format!("{name} is not installed in this store")))
    }

**NOT READY**

- **[P1] Newly backfilled references can lose parent-lifetime protection during narrowing.** [narrowed.rs:139](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-b17-mig/snapback4/crates/snapback4-core/src/store/narrowed.rs:139) archives the transformed row using the predecessor definition and captures references only from that definition’s columns. A newly added reference output therefore gets no dormant parent binding; [backfills.rs:190](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-b17-mig/snapback4/crates/snapback4-core/src/store/backfills.rs:190) captures only argument references.

  Reproduction: add `link: parents` through a point-lookup backfill while narrowing an unrelated child column so the transformed child becomes dormant. Delete and recreate the selected parent with the same id, then widen the child column while carrying the declaration. Re-entry computes the same reference id and restores the child against the replacement parent lifetime without refusing.

  Capture newly produced references using candidate output types when archiving transformed rows, preserving existing invalidation evidence. Add a regression covering this sequence with no reference-typed backfill arguments.
